Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the euclidean framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 933. The resulting operator achieves bound O(529/n^4).

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_933(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 933. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^529(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Statement: For any euclidean module M satisfying the invariance principle hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 807. The projective resolution has length at most 278 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

For the inductive step, we decompose the euclidean structure using the invariance principle theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 278. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
